Such a <sty c="000000" i="1">model </sty>(<sty c="0000d4" i="0">kano>na</sty>) does the apostle prescribe to the ministers of the word, who are to build the church “according to the pattern shewn to them.” (<sty c="800000" i="0"><580805></sty>Hebrews 8:5.)

[BODY] Faithful and upright teachers, and all who allow themselves to conform to this rule, must derive singular encouragement from this passage, in which God, by the mouth of Paul, pronounces on them a blessing. We have no cause to dread the thunders of the Pope, if God promises to us from heaven <sty c="000000" i="1">peace</sty> and <sty c="000000" i="1">mercy</sty>. The word <sty c="000000" i="1">walk</sty> may apply both to a minister and to his

--- PAGE 164 ---

[BODY] people, though it refers chiefly to ministers. The future tense of the verb, (<sty c="0000d4" i="0">o[soi stoich>sousin</sty>,) as many as <sty c="000000" i="1">shall walk</sty>, is intended to express perseverance.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="1">And upon the Israel of God</sty><sty c="000000" i="1">.</sty><sty c="800000" i="0"> F101</sty> This is an indirect ridicule of the vain boasting of the false apostles, who vaunted of being the descendants of Abraham according to the flesh. There are two classes who bear this name, a pretended Israel, which appears to be so in the sight of men, — and the Israel of God. Circumcision was a disguise before men, but regeneration is a truth before God. In a word, he gives the appellation of the <sty c="800000" i="1">Israel of God</sty> to those whom he formerly denominated the children of Abraham by faith, (<sty c="800000" i="0"><480329></sty>Galatians 3:29,) and thus includes all believers, whether Jews or Gentiles, who were united into one church. On the contrary, the name and lineage are the sole boast of Israel according to the flesh; and this led the apostle to argue in the Epistle to the Romans, that “they are not all Israel which are of Israel, neither because they are the seed of Abraham, are they all children.” (<sty c="800000" i="0"><450906></sty>Romans 9:6, 7.)

[BODY] 17. <sty c="800000" i="1">Let no man trouble me</sty>. He now speaks with the voice of authority for restraining his adversaries, and employs language which his high rank fully authorized. “Let them cease to throw hinderances in the course of my preaching.” He was prepared, for the sake of the church, to encounter difficulties, but does not choose to be interrupted by contradiction. <sty c="800000" i="1">Let no</sty> <sty c="800000" i="1">man trouble me</sty>. Let no man make opposition to obstruct the progress of my work.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="1">As to everything else,</sty> (<sty c="0000d4" i="0">tou~ loipou~</sty>,) that is, as to everything besides the <sty c="000000" i="1">new creature</sty>. “This one thing is enough for me. Other matters are of no importance, and give me no concern. Let no man question me about them.” He thus places himself above all men, and allows to none the power of attacking his ministry. Literally, the phrase signifies, as to <sty c="000000" i="1">the rest</sty> or <sty c="000000" i="1">the</sty> <sty c="000000" i="1">remainder</sty>, which Erasmus, in my opinion, has improperly applied to time.
[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="1">For I bear</sty><sty c="800000" i="0"> f102</sty><sty c="800000" i="1"> in my body the marks of the Lord Jesus</sty>. This accounts for his bold, authoritative language. And what were those marks? Imprisonment, chains, scourging, blows, stoning, and every kind of injurious treatment which he had incurred in bearing testimony to the gospel. Earthly warfare has its honors, in conferring which a general holds out to public view the bravery of a soldier. So Christ our leader has his own marks, of which he makes abundant use, for conferring on some of his followers a high

--- PAGE 165 ---

[BODY] distinction. These marks, however, differ from the other in one important respect, that they partake of the nature of the cross, and in the sight of the world are disgraceful. This is suggested by the word translated <sty c="800000" i="1">marks</sty>, (<sty c="0000d4" i="0">sti>gmata</sty>,) for it literally denotes the <sty c="800000" i="1">marks</sty> with which barbarian slaves, or fugitives, or malefactors, were usually branded. Paul, therefore, can hardly be said to use a figure, when he boasts of shining in those marks with which Christ is accustomed to honor his most distinguished soldiers,<sty c="800000" i="0"> f103</sty>
[BODY] which in the eye of the world were attended by shame and disgrace, but which before God and the angels surpass all the honors of the world.<sty c="800000" i="0"> F104</sty>

[BODY] 18. <sty c="800000" i="1">The grace</sty><sty c="800000" i="0"> f105</sty><sty c="800000" i="1"> of our Lord Jesus Christ be with your spirit</sty>. His prayer is not only that God may bestow upon them his grace in large measure, but that they may have a proper feeling of it in their hearts. Then only is it truly enjoyed by us, when it comes to our <sty c="800000" i="1">spirit</sty>. We ought therefore to entreat that God would prepare in our souls a habitation for his grace. Amen.

[CENTERED] END OF THE COMMENTARIES ON THE EPISTLE TO THE GALATIANS.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">6</sty> I wonder that ye are so soon removed from Christ who called you in grace,<sty c="800000" i="0"> f106</sty> to another gospel;

[CENTERED_H2] <sty c="006411" i="0">CHAPTER 3</sty>

[CENTERED_H2] <sty c="006411" i="0">CHAPTER 4</sty>

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">21</sty> Tell me, ye who wish to be under the law, do ye not hear the law?

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">24</sty> Which things are allegorical<sty c="800000" i="0"> f127</sty> for there are two covenants; one indeed from mount Sinai, which gendereth to bondage, that is Agar.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">31</sty> Wherefore, brethren, we are not children of the bond-woman, but of the free.

[CENTERED_H2] <sty c="006411" i="0">CHAPTER 5</sty>

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">1</sty> Stand fast, therefore, in the liberty with which Christ hath made us free; and be not again entangled by the yoke of bondage.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">2</sty> Behold, I Paul protest to you, that, if ye be circumcised, Christ will profit you nothing.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">3</sty> For I testify again to every man who is circumcised that he is a debtor to do the whole law.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">4</sty> Whosoever of you are justified by the law, ye have separated yourselves from Christ; ye are fallen from grace.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">5</sty> For we, through the Spirit, by faith, wait for the hope of righteousness.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">6</sty> For in Christ Jesus neither circumcision availeth any thing, nor uncircumcision; but faith working by love.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">7</sty> Ye were running well. Who hindered you, that ye should not obey the truth?

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">8</sty> This persuasion is not from him who called you.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">9</sty> A little leaven leaveneth the whole lump.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">10</sty> I am persuaded concerning you in the Lord, that ye will think nothing else; <sty c="800000" i="0">f128</sty> but he that troubleth you shall bear the condemnation, whoever he be.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">11</sty> And I, <sty c="800000" i="0">f129</sty> brethren, if I still preach circumcision, why do I still suffer persecution? The offense of the cross is abolished.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">12</sty> Would that they were even cut off who trouble you!

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">13</sty> For ye, brethren, have been called to liberty; only make not the liberty an occasion to the flesh,<sty c="800000" i="0"> f130</sty> but by love serve one another.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">14</sty> For the whole law is fulfilled in one word, namely, this: Thou shalt love thy neighbor as thyself.

--- PAGE 176 ---

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">15</sty> But if ye bite and devour one another, see that ye be not consumed by one another.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">16</sty> Now I say: Walk in the Spirit, and ye shall not fulfill the lust of the flesh.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">17</sty> For the flesh lusteth against the Spirit; and the Spirit against the flesh; and these are contrary to one another, so that ye cannot do those things which ye would wish.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">18</sty> But if ye are led by the Spirit, ye are not under the law.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">19</sty> Now the works of the flesh are manifest, which are adultery, fornication, uncleanness, lasciviousness,

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">20</sty> Idolatry, witchcraft, enmities, strife, emulations, wrath, quarrellings, seditions, heresies,

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">21</sty> Envyings, murders, drunkenness, revellings, and such like; of which I foretell to you, as I also have foretold, that they who do such things shall not inherit the kingdom of God.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">22</sty> But the fruit of the Spirit is love, joy, peace, long-suffering,<sty c="800000" i="0"> f131</sty>
[INDENT] gentleness, kindness, fidelity,

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">23</sty> Meekness, temperance; against such there is no law.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">24</sty> And they who are Christ’s have crucified the flesh with the affections and lusts.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">25</sty> If we live by the Spirit, let us also walk by the Spirit.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">26</sty> Let us not be desirous of vain-glory, provoking one another, envying one another.

[CENTERED_H2] <sty c="006411" i="0">CHAPTER 6</sty>

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">1</sty> Brethren, although a man be overtaken in any fault, ye, who are spiritual, restore such a man in the spirit of meekness; considering thyself, lest thou also be tempted.

[CENTERED] <sty c="800000" i="0">2</sty> Bear ye one another’s burdens, and thus fulfill the law of Christ.

--- PAGE 177 ---

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">3</sty> For if any one thinketh himself to be something, though he is nothing, he deceiveth himself.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">4</sty> But let every one prove his own work; and then shall he have glory in himself alone, and not in another.<sty c="800000" i="0"> F132</sty>

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">5</sty> For every one shall hear his own burden.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">6</sty> Let him who is taught in the word communicate to the teacher in all good things.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">7</sty> Do not mistake: God is not mocked; for what a man shall have sowed, that will he also reap.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">8</sty> For he who soweth to his flesh, shall of the flesh reap corruption; but he who soweth to the Spirit, shall of the Spirit reap everlasting life.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">9</sty> And let us not weary in doing good; for if we do not faint, we shall reap in due time.<sty c="800000" i="0"> F133</sty>

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">10</sty> Therefore, while we have opportunity, let us do good to all, but especially to them who are of the household of faith.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">11</sty> Ye see what a letter<sty c="800000" i="0"> f134</sty> I have written with my own hand.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">12</sty> As many as wish to please outwardly in the flesh<sty c="800000" i="0"> f135</sty> they constrain you to be circumcised; only that they may not suffer persecution for the cross of Christ.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">13</sty> For neither they who hold by circumcision do themselves keep the law; but they wish you to be circumcised, that they may glory in your flesh.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">14</sty> But far be it from me to glory, unless in the cross of our Lord Jesus Christ, by which the world is crucified to me, and I to the world.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">15</sty> For in Christ Jesus neither circumcision availeth anything nor uncircumcision; but a new creature.

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">16</sty> And as many as walk by this rule, peace (be) on them, and mercy, and on the Israel of God.

--- PAGE 178 ---

[BODY] <sty c="800000" i="0">17</sty> Henceforth let no man give me annoyance; for I bear in my body the marks of the Lord Jesus.

[CENTERED] <sty c="800000" i="0">18</sty> The grace of our Lord Jesus Christ (be) with your spirit. Amen.

[FOOTNOTE] <sty c="800000" i="0">Ft10</sty> A forest in Germany, which Caesar describes to be nine days’ journey
[BODY] in breadth, and, at least, sixty days’ journey in length. How much more he was unable to say, as he had never found any person who had traveled farther, or could tell where the forest terminated. He regrets the necessity of employing these vague terms, having placed little reliance on the skill or accuracy of his informers. It is mentioned, he adds, by Eratosthenes and other Greek writers, under the name of <sty c="000000" i="1">Orcynia</sty>. — <sty c="000000" i="1">Ed.</sty>

[FOOTNOTE] <sty c="800000" i="0">Ft22</sty> “<sty c="0000d4" i="0">  jAna>qema</sty>. This word, which we render <sty c="000000" i="1">accursed</sty>, doth not signify

[FOOTNOTE] <sty c="800000" i="0">Ft25</sty> “<sty c="0000d4" i="0">Pei>qw</sty>. This word, which we render <sty c="000000" i="1">persuade</sty>, frequently signifies ‘to
[BODY] obtain by treaty,’ or, ‘to endeavor the friendship and good will of any person.’ Thus in <sty c="800000" i="0"><402814></sty>Matthew 28:14, the chief-priests tell the soldiers, whom they corrupted, to give a false report: ‘If this come to the governor’s ears, we will <sty c="000000" i="1">persuade</sty> him, and secure you, that is, prevail with him to be favorable to you, and save you from punishment.’ Thus, <sty c="800000" i="0"><441220></sty>Acts 12:20, <sty c="0000d4" i="0">pei>santev Bla>ston</sty>, we render, ‘having made Blastus their friend.’ Vid. Pind. Ol. 3:28. And in the Apocryphal book of Maccabees, (2 Maccabees 4:45,) when Menelaus found himself convicted of his crimes, he promised Ptolemy a large sum of money,<sty c="0000d4" i="0">pei~sai to<n basile>a</sty>, ‘to pacify the king,’ to

--- PAGE 182 ---

[BODY] prevent his displeasure, and secure his favor. And thus, in the place before us, ‘to persuade God,’ is to endeavor to secure his approbation; which, the Apostle assures the Galatians, was his great and only view, as well as his great support, under the censure and displeasure of men, for preaching the pure and uncorrupted doctrines of the gospel.” — Chandler.

[FOOTNOTE] <sty c="800000" i="0">Ft30</sty> “The expression, ‘flesh and blood,’ is used to denote men. Thus when
[BODY] Peter confessed to our Lord, ‘Thou art Christ, the Son of the living God,’ Jesus answered, ‘Flesh and blood hath not revealed it unto thee.’ (<sty c="800000" i="0"><401617></sty>Matthew 16:17.) That is, no man hath made this discovery; and thus it hath the same meaning in the place before us. But as the apostle speaks of his countrymen and equals in age, in the verses before, I apprehend he particularly means them, and that he intends to assure the Galatians, that, notwithstanding his former zeal for the law and the traditions of the Jews, yet that, after his extraordinary conversion, he had no longer any dependence on them, nor sought the least direction from the wisest among them.” — Chandler.

[FOOTNOTE] <sty c="800000" i="0">Ft46</sty> “From this portion of sacred history, we are not at liberty to conclude
[BODY] that either of those two apostles had fallen into error in faith; or that they differed from each other about doctrine. Unquestionably, so far as relates to doctrine, Peter was of the same opinion with Paul on this subject, that it was lawful for a Jew to live on terms of friendship with believing Gentiles. — The whole of this controversy related, not to the doctrine of Christian liberty, but to the exercise of it at different times and places; and on this point the rules of prudence were better understood by Paul than by Peter.” — Witsius.
[FOOTNOTE] <sty c="800000" i="0">Ft47 </sty>See Calvin on <sty c="800000" i="0"><480311></sty>Galatians 3:11.
[FOOTNOTE] <sty c="800000" i="0">Ft48</sty> “The Papists will readily acknowledge that we are justified by faith;
[BODY] but they add that it is in part. Now this gloss spoils all; for they are convinced that we cannot be righteous before God, unless it be accomplished by our Lord Jesus Christ, and unless we rely on that salvation which he has procured for us. The Papists see this very well; and therefore, with a careless air, they will say, We are justified by faith. But by faith alone? No. On this point they give battle, and this is the chief article on which we differ from them.” — Calvin’s Sermons.

[CENTERED_H2] <sty c="006411" i="0">CHAPTER 3</sty>

[FOOTNOTE] <sty c="800000" i="0">Ft54</sty> “<sty c="0000d4" i="0">Baskai>nein</sty>, ‘to enchant, to fascinate, to delude by magical charms,’
[BODY] — -rather an uncommon word, <sty c="0000d4" i="0">a{pax lego>menon</sty> in the New Testament. It may amuse to notice the etumon of the word. Some grammarians have strangely thought it derived from <sty c="0000d4" i="0">fa>esi kai>nein</sty>, ‘to kill with the eyes.’ Its true etymology obviously is, <sty c="0000d4" i="0">ba>w, ba>skw,</sty> <sty c="0000d4" i="0">baska>w baskai>nw. ba>skw</sty> (equivalent to <sty c="0000d4" i="0">fa>skw</sty>,), ‘to say, to speak,’ comes, in the form <sty c="0000d4" i="0">baskai>nw</sty>, to signify <sty c="0000d4" i="0">kakologei~n</sty>, ‘to calumniate,’ then ‘to deceive,’ then ‘to deceive by magical arts.’” — Brown.

[FOOTNOTE] <sty c="800000" i="0">Ft62</sty> “This is confessedly one of the most obscure passages in the New

--- PAGE 187 ---

[BODY] (Bloomfield.) Schott remarks, that the bare fact of upwards of 250 interpretations makes it impossible to deny that some obscurity attaches to the Apostle’s language in this passage, arising chiefly from mere brevity of style, but judiciously adds, that, had there not been many commentators more eager to bring forward anything that has the appearance of novelty, than to investigate the ordinary meaning of the terms, the scope of the passage, and the doctrinal statements and reasonings contained in the writings of the Apostle Paul, the interpretations would never have swelled to so large an amount. — <sty c="000000" i="1">Ed</sty>.

[FOOTNOTE] <sty c="800000" i="0">Ft75</sty> “A cataehresis borrows the name of one thing to express another;
[BODY] which thing, though it has a name of its own, yet, under a borrowed name, surprises us with novelty, or infuses into our discourses a bold and daring energy. The Sacred Scriptures will furnish us with many instances of this trope. <sty c="800000" i="0"><032630></sty>Leviticus 26:30, — ‘And I will cast your carcases upon the carcases of your idols;’ that is, upon the ruins of your idols, which shall be as much destroyed as the body is when it is slain, and become a dead carcase. [FOOTNOTE] <sty c="800000" i="0"><198005></sty>Psalm 80:5; <sty c="800000" i="0"><281402></sty>Hosea 14:2. But the boldest catachresis,
[BODY] perhaps, in all the Holy Scriptures, is in <sty c="800000" i="0"><460125></sty>1 Corinthians 1:25., Because the foolishness of God,’ says the apostle, ‘is wiser than men, and the weakness of God is stronger than men;’ that is, what men are apt to account foolishess in God surpasses their wisdom, and what they may be ready to misconstrue as weakness in God, excels all their power. Gibbons’s Rhetoric.

[FOOTNOTE] <sty c="800000" i="0">Ft87</sty> “But I am so far from inculcating on you the necessity of circumcision,

This being the plain and natural sense of the apostle’s words, they cannot be charged with any ill-natured or unfriendly wish.” — Chandler.
[FOOTNOTE] <sty c="800000" i="0">Ft88</sty> The College of the <sty c="000000" i="1">Sorbonne</sty>, in Paris, takes its name from <sty c="000000" i="1">Robert de</sty>
[BODY] <sty c="000000" i="1">Serbonne</sty>, who founded it in the middle of the thirteenth century. Its reputation for theological learning, philosophy, classical literature, and all that formerly constituted a liberal education, was deservedly high. In the Doctors of the Sorbonne the Reformation found powerful adversaries. The very name of this university, to which the greatest scholars in Europe were accustomed to pay deference, would be regarded by the multitude with blind veneration. If such men as Calvin, Beza, Melanchthon, and Luther, were prepared by talents and acquirements of the first order to brave the terrors of that name, they

--- PAGE 193 ---

[BODY] must have frequently lamented its influence on many of their hearers. Yet our author meets undaunted this formidable array, and enters the field with the full assurance of victory. Despising, as we naturally do, the weak superstitions and absurd tenets held by the Church of Rome, we are apt to underrate our obligations to the early champions of the Reformed faith, who encountered, with success, those veteran warriors, and ‘contended earnestly for the faith which was once delivered to the saints.’ (<sty c="800000" i="0"><650103></sty>Jude 1:3.)” — <sty c="000000" i="1">Ed</sty>.

[FOOTNOTE] <sty c="800000" i="0">Ft90</sty> “The original word <sty c="0000d4" i="0">farmakei>a</sty> sometimes denotes ‘poisonings,’
[BODY] which were frequently practised among the heathens. Sometimes it signifies incantations or magic arts, or witchcraft, by which impostors and cheats endeavoured to impose on ignorant and credulous people, and which were carried on by poisonous intoxicating draughts and ointments, by which they did great mischief to the bodies of men. As it is here immediately placed after idolatry, I should imagine that the apostle intended those cursed arts of incantations and charms, those various methods of imposture and cheats, which were made use of by the heathen priests, to promote the idolatrous reverence and worship of their false gods. (See <sty c="800000" i="0"><661823></sty>Revelation 18:23.)” — Chandler.

[FOOTNOTE] <sty c="800000" i="0">Ft95</sty> “I observe an agreement in a somewhat peculiar rule of Christian
[BODY] conduct, as laid down in this epistle, and as exemplified in the Second Epistle to the Corinthians. It is not the repetition of the same general precept, which would have been a coincidence of little value; but it is the general precept in one place, and the application of that precept to an actual occurrence in the other. (See <sty c="800000" i="0"><470206></sty>2 Corinthians 2:6-8.) I have little doubt but that it was the same mind which dictated these two passages.” Paley’s Horae Paulinae.

[BODY] teachers.
